## Previous completed thermoelastic stage

**2026-09-27 thermoelastic inference test completed:** The
[new report](thermoelastic-equivalence/report.md) proves that matching loaded
elongation alone—even over the whole constructed continuous heating-state
path—does not guarantee matching restrained force. A one-load match plus
equal compliance, or two distinct matched load responses at the same thermal
state, does transfer the response within the declared linear model. The
synthetic constitutive law and absence of a heat-flow solution remain explicit.

## Scientifically material limits identified

The comparator expansion's [validation](comparator-expansion/validation.md) records nine admitted PDF baselines rechecked and 53 Windsor page renders reproduced byte-for-byte. A failed expectation of 54 was corrected after independent PDF page counts established 40+13; no page pins changed. Root and separate computational source review checked decisive original pages. The final matrix addressed the review's one missing-field clarification. All 62 receipt rows and 43 local links checked successfully; the record validator passed. None of these checks is a physical model reproduction or licensed expert review. The held HBI download remains uninspected and excluded; new feedback delivery failed because the Sherlock task is archived.

- Several report photographs are timed partly from comparisons of fire distribution or assumed burn duration. Main-agent inspection of NCSTAR 1-9 physical pp. 257 and 274–277 confirms that this is a documented procedure, not a new independent clock measurement. Do not validate the same assumed progression with those inferred times. This limitation does not establish deliberate manipulation or invalidate independently timed images.
- Main-agent visual inspection of physical pp. 278–279 confirms that elevated and street-level views reveal different flame-like regions in a narrow frontage, with report-attributed clocks and floor labels. These are overlapping, not demonstrably simultaneous or independent of NIST's input set. Images are intensity-adjusted/cropped report reproductions, not native originals. Exterior absence claims need viewpoint/obstruction limits.
- The first structured batch makes this concrete: clearer east views contain large irregular luminous forms consistent with flames; lower/south and wide views are often non-evaluable. Bright color is not a thermometer, and no-flame appearance is not no interior heating. Figure5-137's detailed rows are attributed to Floors8/7, not12/13. Its glow-versus-actual-flame distinction needs adjacent footage before any stronger interpretation.
- Five of37 primary labels differ, but geometry/quality field use creates much wider eligibility disagreement. Main has22 candidate openings eligible at25/50% and8 at75% in the close-up; the reviewer's denominator is empty because every unit carries caution/exclusion fields, while its seven flame-positive units across both images remain visible in raw results. Neither is a calibrated burning-window fraction. Version caution/severity/hard-exclusion semantics and human review before consequential counts. Three latent bookkeeping contract gaps require hardening before new inputs; current small, correctly typed, fully in-frame candidate geometry does not exercise them.
- Main primary-page review confirms prescribed/interpolated window breakage, uncertain2MW/noon initialization and a second specified Floor8 fire, Floor12 histories shifted1h/0.5h to represent Floors11/13, and admitted longer north-frontage burning. Some sensitivity tests were reported; it is incorrect to say none existed. Matching spread rate does not independently bound integrated heat or structural effects. This batch neither quantifies the error nor independently supports the source's characterization of the heating overestimate as mild; executable histories and thermal/structural mapping remain required.
- The current selected camera files can support further measurement only with access-copy provenance warnings. Sparse contact sheets are screening material, not acceleration measurements. Camera 2's frame intervals are 99/100/104 ticks at 1/2997 s per tick; this is not a finding of dropped frames or a mechanism discriminator.
- The source inventory does not independently reverify the comparator cause/method labels, reconstruct original media custody, or reproduce five historical analysis derivatives. These are explicit next checks, not reasons to silently discard or double-count the material.
- The initial three-case comparator audit and subsequent [expanded assessment](comparator-expansion/report.md) narrow part of that gap. HBI's named mechanical briquette, explosive reactor and cut-and-pull gas-plant operations now have participant-origin method accounts; native recording/as-executed crosswalks remain incomplete. Two Plasco full manuscripts establish thermal-model scope/calibration and retained non-spread cases. The thesis Chapter 6 audit retains prescribed corner-floor loss, a mismatch and proposed thermal recalibration. Chapter 7 is now fully read: rigid connections and static analysis remain, a revised southeast-floor-removal geometry is prescribed, and the author limits that calculation to initiation. Later video interpretations do not turn it into a dynamic collapse reproduction. Older structural papers and the 2025 journal follow-up remain abstract-level; journal/thesis equivalence and exact executable cases are unresolved. Survival-case suppression, unknown load/restraint matching and Windsor's post-fire sampling/thermal assumptions remain material. Original manifest bytes remain the historical WP0 baseline.
- WP5 source reading establishes a published local metallurgical examination and documented souvenir-handling misconduct without connecting either to a collapse operation. Sample identity, alteration timing and structural relevance are separate missing links. The original OIG scan resolves HTML date/measurement errors and an omitted chart. Official Appendix D resolves the earlier copy's missing spreadsheet pages, not a historical disappearance. The later NIST received-item inventory materially advances receipt for A/W14A and B/W14B while leaving their origin unidentified; repeated tables are one inventory and physical-item continuity remains B-grade. C12a/b receipt/renumbering, any Sample 1/coupon join, candidate-specific attribution rationale, original photos and full methods remain unresolved. DOC-NIST-2013-001311 is a request-log locator, not a response/outcome.
- Camera 3's matching result is algorithmically censored: stopping at frame 200 does not mark physical support gain or disappearance. Penthouse matching failure is not a collapse-onset measurement. High correlation and overlapping-template agreement cannot certify physical-point identity. Translation-only reference agreement is not target uncertainty or metric calibration.
- Consecutive-frame review now directly shows continued visible roof descent after that stop, followed by occlusion/identity ambiguity. Recurrent near-repeat content is numerically established in the access copy; original exposure cadence, conversion history and any corrected time model remain unresolved. No frames or PTS were altered. The charter's small human landmark spot-check was requested asynchronously and remains unanswered; no broader human approval can be inferred from it.
- The longer newly acquired kit media do not yet resolve acquisition cadence. Their extra frames, shared nominal 15 fps and saved three-frame project step must not be turned into an assumed inter-file mapping. NIST's Appendix C instead documents a 29.97-fps broadcast-VHS-to-DV import; problems in the current short clip cannot simply be imputed to that input. ## 2026-09-08 — Local Sherlock runtime adoption

The earlier statements above that no real Sherlock case had been initialized describe the pre-adoption state and remain as history. A project-owned [local integration](sherlock-integration/README.md) now uses pinned Sherlock **0.1.2** with a fresh research-only workspace. Its structured charter preserves the complete original `CHARTER.md` text exactly as scope, together with explicit constraints and review boundaries. Q01–Q10 are initialized as ten **open questions**, with their charter discriminators retained as material-change conditions.

This adoption imports scope and question records only. It imports no evidence files or research reports and creates no claims, accepted findings, publication, or canonical promotion. Report generation and automatic downloads are disabled in this project's workbench. The runtime uses an agent identity; review acceptance and publication authority are not conferred by initialization. The integration guide records the launcher, pinned runtime and local verification commands.

`CHARTER.md` remains the controlling scientific charter; its original bytes, WP0 inventory/report and prior method outputs are preserved. Conforming case setup is now available, superseding only that setup gap in the earlier WP0 disposition. Clock/origin reconciliation, calibrated media methods, uncertainty propagation, package completeness, expert review and the remaining scientific work packages are still incomplete. No causal ranking or litigation-record status changes through this software adoption.
